<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Global Spherical Shell conjecture: every connected minimal compact complex surface of class VII with positive second Betti number contains a global spherical shell. This is a holomorphically embedded neighborhood of the standard three-sphere in $`\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\}`$ whose complement is connected.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a connected compact complex surface with $$b_1(X)=1,\qquad b_2(X)>0,\qquad \kappa(X)=-\infty.$$ Assume that $X$ is minimal: it contains no smooth rational curve of self-intersection $-1$. Then there are an open neighborhood $U$ of $$S^3=\{z\in\mathbb C^2:\|z\|=1\} \quad\text{in }\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\},$$ an open subset $\Sigma\subset X$, and a biholomorphism $\phi:U\longrightarrow\Sigma$ such that $X\setminus\Sigma$ is connected.
  - Explanation: Every surface satisfying the stated class VII and minimality hypotheses has a global spherical shell with connected complement.
- **C2** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-topology)): Let $X$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, and put $b=b_2(X)$. There is a small one-parameter deformation $\mathcal X\to\Delta$ over a disk about $0$, with $\mathcal X_0\simeq X$, such that every nonzero fiber is obtained from a primary Hopf surface by exactly $b$ successive point blowups. The centers may be infinitely near. Moreover, $$\pi_1(X)\cong\mathbb Z,\qquad X\cong_{\mathrm{or}}(S^1\times S^3)\#\,b\,\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{\,2},$$ where the second is an orientation-preserving diffeomorphism for the complex orientation of $X$.
  - Explanation: The shell gives deformation to blown-up primary Hopf surfaces and determines the fundamental group and oriented smooth topology.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-aspherical-geography)): Let $S$ be a connected closed smooth complex surface whose topological universal cover is contractible. Write $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)$ for its Euler characteristic and $\sigma(S)$ for its signature in the complex orientation. Then $$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)\geq\frac95\|\sigma(S)\|.$$ If $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)>0$, then $S$ is of general type; otherwise $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)=\sigma(S)=0$.
  - Explanation: For aspherical complex surfaces the Euler characteristic bounds the absolute signature, and positive Euler characteristic forces general type.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-surface-reduction)): Let $V$ be a minimal compact complex surface with $b_1(V)=1$, $\kappa(V)=-\infty$, and $b_2(V)>0$.* 1. *If $V$ has a nonzero effective divisor $C$ with $C^2=0$, then $V$ has a global spherical shell.* 2. *If $V$ has exactly $b_2(V)$ rational curves, then $V$ has a global spherical shell.* *Here the conclusion supplies an embedded open neighborhood of $S^3\subset\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\}$ with connected complement in $V$.
  - Explanation: An effective square-zero divisor or the stated rational-curve count supplies a shell under the surface hypotheses.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-surface-degree)): Put $b=b_2(X)$. There is a basis $u_1,\ldots,u_b$ of $H^2(X;\mathbb Z)/\operatorname{Tors}$ with $$\label{surface:canonical-basis} u_i\cdot u_j=-\delta_{ij},\qquad K_X=u_1+\cdots+u_b\pmod{\operatorname{Tors}}.$$ For every curve $B\subset X$, $$\label{surface:curve-degrees} B^2<0,\qquad (K_X+B)\cdot B\leq0,\qquad K_X\cdot B\geq0.$$ Consequently every effective divisor has nonnegative canonical degree.
  - Explanation: The canonical basis diagonalizes the negative intersection form; minimality and adjunction make effective canonical degrees nonnegative.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-surface-characters)): For $\lambda\neq1$ and every $q=0,1,2$, $$\label{surface:acyclic} H^q(X,P_\lambda)=0, \qquad H^q(X,K_X\otimes P_\lambda)=0.$$ For every $\lambda\in\mathbb C^*$, including $\lambda=1$, $$\label{surface:canonical-vanishing} H^0(X,K_X\otimes P_\lambda)=0.$$ At the trivial character, $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X)=\mathbb C$, $H^1(X,\mathcal O_X)=\mathbb C$, and $H^2(X,\mathcal O_X)=0$. If a holomorphic function $f$ on $Y$ satisfies $f\circ T-f=c$ for a constant $c$, then $f$ is constant and $c=0$. More generally, a holomorphic function annihilated by a positive power of $T^*-1$ is constant.
  - Explanation: Nontrivial character bundles are acyclic; canonical character sections and nonconstant generalized additive holomorphic modes are excluded.
- **C7** (construction, [source](#stmt-surface-height)): There are a Gauduchon metric on $X$, a choice of $T$, and a smooth proper height $h:Y\to\mathbb R$ such that $$\label{surface:positive-height} h(Ty)=h(y)+1,\qquad Lh=c>0.$$ Here $L$ is the complex Laplacian with the subharmonic sign. All positive order derivatives of $h$ are uniformly bounded in the lifted metric.
  - Explanation: A lifted Gauduchon metric admits a proper height with positive constant complex Laplacian and bounded positive-order derivatives.
- **C8** (classification, [source](#stmt-surface-covers)): For every $n\geq1$, put $S=T^n$ and $X_n=Y/\langle S\rangle$, and let $p_n:X_n\to X$ be the degree-$n$ covering. Then $X_n$ is a minimal class VII surface and $$\chi(\mathcal O_{X_n})=0,\qquad b_1(X_n)=1,\qquad b_2(X_n)=n b_2(X),\qquad K_{X_n}=p_n^*K_X.$$ In particular its integral intersection lattice modulo torsion has a negative orthonormal basis whose sum is $K_{X_n}$ modulo torsion.
  - Explanation: Finite cyclic quotients preserve minimal class VII structure and scale the intersection lattice with covering degree.
- **C9** (construction, [source](#stmt-compact-classes)): There is a constant $C_0\geq0$, independent of $n$, such that for each $n$ one can choose smooth line-bundle data $E_1,\ldots,E_d$ on $Y$, supported in the interior of $W_n$, with $$\label{compact:rank-bound} d\geq n b_2(X)-C_0.$$ They are obtained by cutting line bundles on $X_n$ that are trivialized near $Z$. Their transferred Chern classes $w_1,\ldots,w_d\in H^2(X_n;\mathbb Z)$ are linearly independent over $\mathbb Q$ modulo torsion. Their compact Chern classes $e_i$ satisfy $$\label{compact:numerical-data} e_i^2=K_Y\cdot e_i=-m_i<0$$ for positive integers $m_i$. Pairings of these compact classes equal the corresponding pairings of the transferred classes on $X_n$.
  - Explanation: Cutting bundles trivialized at a fixed seam produces independent compact classes with equal negative self-intersection and canonical intersection.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weighted-fredholm)): For $p=0$ or $p=2$, every admissible pair $(a,b)$ and every $s\in\mathbb Z$, the complex <a href="#weighted:stepped" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="weighted:stepped">[weighted:stepped]</a> has closed ranges and finite-dimensional cohomology. Its cohomology agrees with that of the maximal weighted $L^2$ complex and is represented by forms which belong to $W^k_{a,b}$ for every $k$. There are bounded homotopies of degree $-1$ which gain one derivative, modulo projections onto these harmonic spaces. For the periodic trivial datum the single-linear-weight complex with $\rho\ne0$ is acyclic, in every Sobolev realization. The operator bounds may depend on the fixed rates and the datum.
  - Explanation: Admissible weights give closed ranges, finite-dimensional smooth cohomology, and derivative-gaining homotopies; nonzero single linear weights are acyclic.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weighted-duality)): For $p\in\{0,2\}$ and $0\leq q\leq2$, the integration pairing gives a perfect pairing $$\label{weighted:serre} H^{p,q}_{a,b}(Y,E)\ \times\ H^{2-p,2-q}_{-a,-b}(Y,E^{-1})\longrightarrow\mathbb C.$$ Here both pairs of rates are admissible. The statement concerns the cohomology classes in any of the stepped realizations above; the pairing is evaluated on their smooth weighted harmonic representatives.
  - Explanation: Integration pairs weighted cohomology perfectly with dual-bundle cohomology at opposite rates.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weighted-nondecay)): Let $V$ be a holomorphic line bundle on $\{h>H_0\}$, with a smooth Hermitian metric of bounded Chern curvature. If a holomorphic section $s$ satisfies, for every $N>0$, $$\|s(x)\|\le C_Ne^{-Nh(x)}\quad(h(x)\text{ sufficiently large}),$$ then $s$ vanishes on a positive tail. On every connected region on which it is holomorphic and which meets that tail it consequently vanishes identically.
  - Explanation: Faster-than-every-exponential decay on a positive tail forces a holomorphic section with bounded curvature to vanish there.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weighted-continuation)): Let $V_0$ be either $\mathcal O_Y$ or $K_Y$ with its periodic holomorphic operator $d$. Suppose on one tail that $$(d+B\wedge)s=0,\qquad \|\nabla^jB\|\le C_j e^{-\gamma\|h\|},\quad j\ge0,$$ where the perturbed operator is integrable and $\gamma>0$. If $s$ has finite exponential growth, its allowed rate can be improved by any amount strictly less than $\gamma$, except that a contour passing the unit multiplier can contribute a constant when $V_0=\mathcal O_Y$. There is no degree-zero contribution for $V_0=K_Y$.* *In the scalar case, after finitely many such improvements there is a constant $c_\pm$ on the relevant end and an exponentially decaying remainder, with decay in all derivatives. If that constant is zero, the section has every finite exponential decay rate on that end. In the canonical case it always has every finite exponential decay rate.
  - Explanation: Contour continuation improves allowed rates; only scalar constants contribute when the unit multiplier is crossed.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weighted-tail)): Let $E$ satisfy <a href="#weighted:end-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="weighted:end-error">[weighted:end-error]</a>. A finite-growth holomorphic section of $K_Y\otimes E$ or $K_Y\otimes E^{-1}$ on a positive tail vanishes on a shorter positive tail. A finite-growth holomorphic section of $E$ has, in the prescribed trivialization at either end on which it is defined, the expansion $$s=c_\pm+O(e^{-\delta \|h\|})$$ for some $\delta>0$, with the same conclusion for every derivative of the error. On the positive end $c_+=0$ implies $s=0$. Consequently the positive-end coefficient is injective on global weighted holomorphic sections of $E$.
  - Explanation: Canonical sections vanish on positive tails, while scalar-type sections have constant end coefficients and positive-end uniqueness.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weighted-scalar-top)): For the scalar periodic Dolbeault complex and every admissible pair, $$H^{0,2}_{a,b}(Y,\mathcal O)=0.$$ In particular, if $f$ is a smooth compactly supported $(0,2)$-form and $R>0$, there is a smooth $(0,1)$-form $\alpha$ with $\bar\partial\alpha=f$ and $\alpha\in W^k_{-R,-R}$ for every $k\in\mathbb Z$.
  - Explanation: Scalar top-degree vanishing supplies rapidly decaying primitives for compactly supported forms.
- **C16** (construction, [source](#stmt-weighted-holomorphic)): Every compact line-bundle datum admits an integrable $(0,1)$-connection satisfying <a href="#weighted:end-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="weighted:end-error">[weighted:end-error]</a> with any prescribed finite $\gamma>0$. For every such structure and every admissible pair, $$\label{weighted:E-top} H^{0,2}_{a,b}(Y,E)=0.$$ For every $a,b>0$, global weighted holomorphic sections have the two constant end coefficients and the exponentially decaying errors described in Proposition <a href="#weighted:tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="weighted:tail">15</a>; the map $s\mapsto c_+$ is injective.
  - Explanation: Compact end-framed data admit holomorphic structures with any prescribed finite correction rate, top-degree vanishing, and injective positive coefficient.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weighted-base-index)): For the trivial datum and $a,b>0$, $$H^{0,0}_{a,b}(Y,\mathcal O)=\mathbb C, \qquad H^{0,1}_{a,b}(Y,\mathcal O)=H^{0,2}_{a,b}(Y,\mathcal O)=0.$$ In particular its weighted Dolbeault index is one.
  - Explanation: The trivial datum has only constant sections at positive end rates and has vanishing higher weighted cohomology.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weighted-index)): Let $E$ be a compact datum supported in a cut-open period of a finite cyclic quotient $X_n=Y/T^n$, with its trivializations fixed in collars of the cut. Equip $E$ with an integrable operator $d_E$ satisfying <a href="#weighted:end-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="weighted:end-error">[weighted:end-error]</a>, and let $e$ be its compact support Chern class. For $a,b>0$ its weighted Dolbeault index is $$\label{weighted:index-formula} \sum_{q=0}^2(-1)^q\dim H^{0,q}_{a,b}(Y,E) =1+\frac12\bigl(e^2-K_Y\cdot e\bigr).$$ The products on the right are compact support intersection pairings. In particular, if $e^2=K_Y\cdot e$, the section space is one-dimensional, its positive-end coefficient is nonzero on every nonzero section, and $H^{0,1}_{a,b}(Y,E)=0$.
  - Explanation: Compact excision computes the weighted index from compact intersection products; equality of the products forces a unique normalized section.
- **C19** (construction, [source](#stmt-compact-section)): Let $E$ be one of the data in Lemma <a href="#compact:classes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="compact:classes">9</a>, and write $e^2=K_Y\cdot e=-m$ with $m>0$. Given any finite $\gamma>0$, $E$ admits a holomorphic structure whose Dolbeault operator, in its fixed end frames, differs from the trivial operator by $O(e^{-\gamma\|h\|})$ in every derivative. For this structure there is a holomorphic section $s$ such that, in those frames, $$\label{compact:end-limits} s\longrightarrow1\quad(h\to+\infty),\qquad s\longrightarrow0\quad(h\to-\infty),$$ with exponentially decaying errors. Its effective divisor $D=(s=0)$ is contained in $\{h\leq H\}$ for some $H$, is locally finite, and represents the ordinary Chern class $j(e)$, or equivalently its dual locally finite homology class.
  - Explanation: The normalized section has unequal end limits. A nonzero negative coefficient would give a compact divisor of negative projected canonical degree, contradicting nonnegative effective canonical degrees.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-curve-area)): There is a number $A>0$, depending only on a fixed finite coordinate atlas of $X$, its lifted metric, and the covering height, with the following property. Let $E$ be any compact datum whose holomorphic operator tends smoothly to the trivial operator at the negative end, and let $s$ be a nonzero holomorphic section which is bounded in the prescribed smooth trivialization there. If $D=(s=0)$, then $$\label{curve:area-bound} \mathop{\mathrm{Area}}(D\cap Y_l)\le C_{E,s}\exp(Al),\qquad l\ge0.$$ The constant $C_{E,s}$ may depend on the datum, operator, and section. The exponent $A$ is independent of their decay rate. The same exponent applies to every reduced component of $D$ and each of its fixed deck translates. Replacing $I_l$ by any fixed-width enlargement also preserves the exponent.
  - Explanation: A common geometric exponent bounds negative-slab divisor areas; individual prefactors may depend on the bundle and section.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-weighted-currents)): Fix $\varepsilon>0$ and $b>\varepsilon$. Let $F$ be either $\mathcal O_Y$ or an end-framed compact datum $E$ with an integrable operator satisfying <a href="#weighted:end-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="weighted:end-error">[weighted:end-error]</a>. Consider the current complex $$\label{weighted:current-complex} \mathcal C^{2,q}_{-\varepsilon,b,-3}(F) =W^{-3-q}_{-\varepsilon,b}(\Lambda^{2,q}\otimes F), \qquad q=0,1,2.$$ Its cohomology is finite-dimensional. For $F=\mathcal O_Y$ its degree-one cohomology is zero. In particular every closed scalar $(2,1)$-current in this space which is zero on a positive tail is exact.* *If a degree-zero current $\beta\in W^{-3}_{-\varepsilon,b}(\Lambda^{2,0}\otimes F)$ satisfies $\bar\partial_F\beta=J$ for a degree-one current $J$ supported in $\{h\le C\}$, then $\beta$ vanishes on a positive tail. Finally, an order-zero $(2,1)$-current supported in $\{h\le C\}$ whose masses on negative unit height slabs are at most $C_0e^{\mu l}$ belongs to this complex whenever $b>\mu$. The same Sobolev order and pair of rates can be used for any family with that common exponent $\mu$, regardless of its individual constants and upper support bounds.
  - Explanation: One fixed weighted current complex has finite-dimensional cohomology and scalar degree-one vanishing; strict domination of the mass exponent ensures membership.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-curve-residue)): Fix $F\in\{\mathcal O,E\}$ and rates $(-\varepsilon,b)$ as in Theorem <a href="#weighted:currents" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="weighted:currents">22</a>. For distinct closed irreducible curves $B_i\subset Y$ bounded above in height, let $\eta_i$ be nonzero holomorphic differentials on their normalizations with coefficients in $f_i^*F$. Suppose their pushforward currents have negative-end mass exponents strictly smaller than $b$. Then the classes $$[C_{\eta_i}]\in H^{2,1}_{-\varepsilon,b}(Y,F)$$ are linearly independent: every finite subfamily is independent. For $F=\mathcal O$, even one such differential is impossible.
  - Explanation: Nonzero differentials on distinct curves give independent residue classes in a fixed weighted space; scalar vanishing rules out even a single such differential.
- **C23** (classification, [source](#stmt-curve-genus)): The normalization $R$ of any component of $D$ has no nonzero square-integrable scalar holomorphic differential and has genus zero. If $R$ is compact, it is biholomorphic to $\mathbb P^1$. If it is noncompact, it is biholomorphic to a domain in $\mathbb P^1$, without a restriction on the connectivity of that domain.
  - Explanation: The scalar residue obstruction excludes square-integrable differentials and forces genus-zero normalizations, allowing arbitrary connectivity in the open case.
- **C24** (bound, [source](#stmt-cylinder-small-energy)): There are an integer $P\geq2$ and positive constants $Q,\delta,\varepsilon_0,D_0$, depending only on the lifted geometry, with the following property. Suppose that $\Omega\subset\mathcal C$ is a domain and $f:\Omega\to Y$ is a nonconstant proper holomorphic map. A strip $S_j$ is called *flat* if $\overline{C_j^{2P+2}}\subset\Omega$ and the image of this entire thickened cylinder under $f$ is contained in $U_p(r)$ for one of the fixed coordinate charts. Every flat strip satisfies $\sup_{S_j}u\leq D_0$.* *For every interval $I\subset\mathbb R$, if $S_j$ contains a point $v_j$ with $h(f(v_j))\in I$ and $S_j$ is not flat, then $$\label{cylinder:energy-cost} \int_{\Omega\cap C_j^Q\cap(h\circ f)^{-1}(I+[-\delta,\delta])} u^2\,dA_v\geq\varepsilon_0.$$ The same assertion holds on a source half-cylinder whenever $\overline{C_j^Q}$ lies in its interior.
  - Explanation: Properness and analytic-cycle compactness force low-energy thickened source cylinders into coordinate charts; nonflat strips have a uniform positive energy cost.
- **C25** (bound, [source](#stmt-cylinder-flat-chains)): Let $J\subset\mathbb Z$ be a maximal interval of flat indices for a map as in Lemma <a href="#cylinder:small-energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cylinder:small-energy">25</a>. Put $m_j=\sup_{S_j}u$. There is a constant $L_0$, depending only on the fixed geometry, such that $$\label{cylinder:chain-sum} \sum_{j\in J}m_j\leq L_0.$$ A doubly infinite chain is impossible for a nonconstant map. If a chain contains a point with height in an interval $I$, every finite endpoint of the chain has a neighboring nonflat strip containing a point with height in $I+[-\Lambda,\Lambda]$, where $\Lambda=\\|dh\\|_\infty L_0$ is fixed.
  - Explanation: Zero angular constant mode gives summable derivative bounds along flat chains and bounded target-height variation, independent of chain length.
- **C26** (bound, [source](#stmt-cylinder-estimate)): There are constants $R_0,C_0>0$ depending only on the lifted geometry with the following property. Let $f:\Omega\to Y$ be a nonconstant proper holomorphic map from a domain in $\mathcal C$. Let $w\geq0$ be measurable and suppose $$M_w:=\sup_{j\in\mathbb Z}\\|w\\|_{L^2(\Omega\cap S_j)}<\infty.$$ For every interval $I$ of length one, set $$E_I=\int_{\Omega\cap(h\circ f)^{-1}(I+[-R_0,R_0])} u^2\,dA_v.$$ If $E_I<\infty$, then $$\label{cylinder:estimate-formula} \int_{\Omega\cap(h\circ f)^{-1}(I)}u\,w\,dA_v \leq C_0M_w(1+E_I).$$ The same estimate holds on a full source half-cylinder cut from a proper normalization map; properness is used only on thickened strips away from the artificial initial boundary, and a fixed finite initial block of strips is included in the constant. The integral on the left is always restricted to the inverse image of the target interval $I$.
  - Explanation: The target-slab estimate combines energy costs on nonflat strips with summable flat-chain derivatives and a stripwise bounded multiplier.
- **C27** (classification, [source](#stmt-cylinder-plane)): If a component of $D$ is noncompact, its normalization is biholomorphic to $\mathbb C$.
  - Explanation: The scalar residue obstruction rules out open planar normalizations omitting distinct sphere points, leaving the plane.
- **C28** (construction, [source](#stmt-cylinder-gauge)): If $a\in L^1(\mathcal C)\cap L^2(\mathcal C)$, there is a periodic distribution $g$ satisfying $$\tfrac12(\partial_t+i\partial_\theta)g=a, \qquad \sup_{j\in\mathbb Z}\\|g\\|_{W^{1,2}(S_j)} \leq C\bigl(\\|a\\|_{L^1}+\\|a\\|_{L^2}\bigr).$$ For every $p<\infty$ this solution satisfies $$\label{cylinder:gauge-exp} \sup_j\int_{S_j}\exp\bigl(p\|\operatorname{Re}g\|\bigr)dA_v<\infty.$$ If $a$ is smooth in an open set, $g$ is smooth there.
  - Explanation: The angular constant mode and the remaining Fourier modes yield a periodic gauge with bounded strip norms and exponential integrability.
- **C29** (classification, [source](#stmt-cylinder-compactness)): Every irreducible component of $D$ is compact and has rational normalization.
  - Explanation: Plane translates produce independent bundle-valued residue classes in a fixed finite-dimensional space, so every divisor component is compact rational.
- **C30** (bound, [source](#stmt-conclusion-lifts)): Every compact rational curve in $Y$ projects to a rational curve in $X$. For each rational curve $B\subset X$, the compact irreducible curves in $Y$ projecting onto $B$ form one orbit under $T$. In particular, a collection of $r$ rational curves in $X$ gives at most $nr$ orbits of compact lifts under $S=T^n$.
  - Explanation: Normalization maps lift rational curves into one deck orbit; singular curves are handled without assuming their full inverse images have compact connected components.
- **C31** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conclusion-pairing-rank)): For the classes $e_1,\ldots,e_d$ of Lemma <a href="#compact:classes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="compact:classes">9</a>, the matrix $P(t)=(P(e_i,e_j))_{i,j=1}^d$ has nonzero determinant in $\Lambda$. Their $\Lambda$-span is free of rank $d$, and $P$ is nonsingular on its extension of scalars to $\mathbb F$.
  - Explanation: Negative definiteness after periodization gives a nonsingular Laurent pairing and independent compact classes over the Laurent ring.
- **C32** (bound, [source](#stmt-conclusion-curves)): The surface $X$ contains at least $b_2(X)$ rational curves.
  - Explanation: Too few rational curves would create a nonzero compact class orthogonal to all divisor components, contradicting the Laurent pairing.
- **C33** (bound, [source](#stmt-conclusion-upper-bound)): Under <a href="#surface:no-square-zero" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="surface:no-square-zero">[surface:no-square-zero]</a>, the real cohomology classes of any finite collection of distinct curves on $X$ are linearly independent. Consequently $X$ has at most $b_2(X)$ curves in total.
  - Explanation: Negative definiteness, local intersection positivity, and the absence of effective square-zero divisors force independence of distinct curve classes.

      "exact_statement": "For $\\lambda\\neq1$ and every $q=0,1,2$, $$\\label{surface:acyclic}\n H^q(X,P_\\lambda)=0,\n \\qquad H^q(X,K_X\\otimes P_\\lambda)=0.$$ For every $\\lambda\\in\\mathbb C^*$, including $\\lambda=1$, $$\\label{surface:canonical-vanishing}\n H^0(X,K_X\\otimes P_\\lambda)=0.$$ At the trivial character, $H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X)=\\mathbb C$, $H^1(X,\\mathcal O_X)=\\mathbb C$, and $H^2(X,\\mathcal O_X)=0$. If a holomorphic function $f$ on $Y$ satisfies $f\\circ T-f=c$ for a constant $c$, then $f$ is constant and $c=0$. More generally, a holomorphic function annihilated by a positive power of $T^*-1$ is constant.",
      "explanation": "Nontrivial character bundles are acyclic; canonical character sections and nonconstant generalized additive holomorphic modes are excluded.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-surface-characters",
          "exact_quote": "For $\\lambda\\neq1$ and every $q=0,1,2$, $$\\label{surface:acyclic}\n H^q(X,P_\\lambda)=0,\n \\qquad H^q(X,K_X\\otimes P_\\lambda)=0.$$ For every $\\lambda\\in\\mathbb C^*$, including $\\lambda=1$, $$\\label{surface:canonical-vanishing}\n H^0(X,K_X\\otimes P_\\lambda)=0.$$ At the trivial character, $H^0(X,\\mathcal O_X)=\\mathbb C$, $H^1(X,\\mathcal O_X)=\\mathbb C$, and $H^2(X,\\mathcal O_X)=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-060.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a connected compact complex surface with $$b_1(X)=1,\\qquad b_2(X)>0,\\qquad \\kappa(X)=-\\infty.$$ Assume that $X$ is minimal: it contains no smooth rational curve of self-intersection $-1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-060.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-surface-height",
        "exact_quote": "There are a Gauduchon metric on $X$, a choice of $T$, and a smooth proper height $h:Y\\to\\mathbb R$ such that $$\\label{surface:positive-height}\n h(Ty)=h(y)+1,\\qquad Lh=c>0.$$ Here $L$ is the complex Laplacian with the subharmonic sign.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-surface-covers",
        "exact_quote": "For every $n\\geq1$, put $S=T^n$ and $X_n=Y/\\langle S\\rangle$, and let $p_n:X_n\\to X$ be the degree-$n$ covering.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-compact-classes",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C_0\\geq0$, independent of $n$, such that for each $n$ one can choose smooth line-bundle data $E_1,\\ldots,E_d$ on $Y$, supported in the interior of $W_n$, with $$\\label{compact:rank-bound}\n d\\geq n b_2(X)-C_0.$$ They are obtained by cutting line bundles on $X_n$ that are trivialized near $Z$. Their transferred Chern classes $w_1,\\ldots,w_d\\in H^2(X_n;\\mathbb Z)$ are linearly independent over $\\mathbb Q$ modulo torsion. Their compact Chern classes $e_i$ satisfy $$\\label{compact:numerical-data}\n e_i^2=K_Y\\cdot e_i=-m_i<0$$ for positive integers $m_i$. Pairings of these compact classes equal the corresponding pairings of the transferred classes on $X_n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-weighted-fredholm",
        "exact_quote": "For $p=0$ or $p=2$, every admissible pair $(a,b)$ and every $s\\in\\mathbb Z$, the complex <a href=\"#weighted:stepped\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weighted:stepped\">[weighted:stepped]</a> has closed ranges and finite-dimensional cohomology. Its cohomology agrees with that of the maximal weighted $L^2$ complex and is represented by forms which belong to $W^k_{a,b}$ for every $k$. There are bounded homotopies of degree $-1$ which gain one derivative, modulo projections onto these harmonic spaces. For the periodic trivial datum the single-linear-weight complex with $\\rho\\ne0$ is acyclic, in every Sobolev realization. The operator bounds may depend on the fixed rates and the datum.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-weighted-nondecay",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a holomorphic line bundle on $\\{h>H_0\\}$, with a smooth Hermitian metric of bounded Chern curvature. If a holomorphic section $s$ satisfies, for every $N>0$, $$|s(x)|\\le C_Ne^{-Nh(x)}\\quad(h(x)\\text{ sufficiently large}),$$ then $s$ vanishes on a positive tail.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-weighted-continuation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V_0$ be either $\\mathcal O_Y$ or $K_Y$ with its periodic holomorphic operator $d$. Suppose on one tail that $$(d+B\\wedge)s=0,\\qquad\n |\\nabla^jB|\\le C_j e^{-\\gamma|h|},\\quad j\\ge0,$$ where the perturbed operator is integrable and $\\gamma>0$. If $s$ has finite exponential growth, its allowed rate can be improved by any amount strictly less than $\\gamma$, except that a contour passing the unit multiplier can contribute a constant when $V_0=\\mathcal O_Y$. There is no degree-zero contribution for $V_0=K_Y$.*\n\n*In the scalar case, after finitely many such improvements there is a constant $c_\\pm$ on the relevant end and an exponentially decaying remainder, with decay in all derivatives. If that constant is zero, the section has every finite exponential decay rate on that end. In the canonical case it always has every finite exponential decay rate.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-weighted-continuation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V_0$ be either $\\mathcal O_Y$ or $K_Y$ with its periodic holomorphic operator $d$. If that constant is zero, the section has every finite exponential decay rate on that end. In the canonical case it always has every finite exponential decay rate.",
          "source_id": "PM-060.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-weighted-tail",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ satisfy <a href=\"#weighted:end-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weighted:end-error\">[weighted:end-error]</a>. A finite-growth holomorphic section of $K_Y\\otimes E$ or $K_Y\\otimes E^{-1}$ on a positive tail vanishes on a shorter positive tail. A finite-growth holomorphic section of $E$ has, in the prescribed trivialization at either end on which it is defined, the expansion $$s=c_\\pm+O(e^{-\\delta |h|})$$ for some $\\delta>0$, with the same conclusion for every derivative of the error. On the positive end $c_+=0$ implies $s=0$. Consequently the positive-end coefficient is injective on global weighted holomorphic sections of $E$.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ satisfy <a href=\"#weighted:end-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weighted:end-error\">[weighted:end-error]</a>.       "exact_statement": "Let $E$ satisfy <a href=\"#weighted:end-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weighted:end-error\">[weighted:end-error]</a>. A finite-growth holomorphic section of $E$ has, in the prescribed trivialization at either end on which it is defined, the expansion $$s=c_\\pm+O(e^{-\\delta |h|})$$ for some $\\delta>0$, with the same conclusion for every derivative of the error.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-weighted-holomorphic",
        "exact_quote": "Every compact line-bundle datum admits an integrable $(0,1)$-connection satisfying <a href=\"#weighted:end-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weighted:end-error\">[weighted:end-error]</a> with any prescribed finite $\\gamma>0$. For every such structure and every admissible pair, $$\\label{weighted:E-top}\n H^{0,2}_{a,b}(Y,E)=0.$$ For every $a,b>0$, global weighted holomorphic sections have the two constant end coefficients and the exponentially decaying errors described in Proposition <a href=\"#weighted:tail\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"weighted:tail\">15</a>; the map $s\\mapsto c_+$ is injective.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-weighted-index",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a compact datum supported in a cut-open period of a finite cyclic quotient $X_n=Y/T^n$, with its trivializations fixed in collars of the cut. Equip $E$ with an integrable operator $d_E$ satisfying <a href=\"#weighted:end-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weighted:end-error\">[weighted:end-error]</a>, and let $e$ be its compact support Chern class. For $a,b>0$ its weighted Dolbeault index is $$\\label{weighted:index-formula}\n \\sum_{q=0}^2(-1)^q\\dim H^{0,q}_{a,b}(Y,E)\n =1+\\frac12\\bigl(e^2-K_Y\\cdot e\\bigr).$$ The products on the right are compact support intersection pairings. In particular, if $e^2=K_Y\\cdot e$, the section space is one-dimensional, its positive-end coefficient is nonzero on every nonzero section, and $H^{0,1}_{a,b}(Y,E)=0$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-compact-section",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be one of the data in Lemma <a href=\"#compact:classes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"compact:classes\">9</a>, and write $e^2=K_Y\\cdot e=-m$ with $m>0$. Given any finite $\\gamma>0$, $E$ admits a holomorphic structure whose Dolbeault operator, in its fixed end frames, differs from the trivial operator by $O(e^{-\\gamma|h|})$ in every derivative. For this structure there is a holomorphic section $s$ such that, in those frames, $$\\label{compact:end-limits}\n s\\longrightarrow1\\quad(h\\to+\\infty),\\qquad\n s\\longrightarrow0\\quad(h\\to-\\infty),$$ with exponentially decaying errors. Its effective divisor $D=(s=0)$ is contained in $\\{h\\leq H\\}$ for some $H$, is locally finite, and represents the ordinary Chern class $j(e)$, or equivalently its dual locally finite homology class.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-compact-section",

Given any finite $\\gamma>0$, $E$ admits a holomorphic structure whose Dolbeault operator, in its fixed end frames, differs from the trivial operator by $O(e^{-\\gamma|h|})$ in every derivative. For this structure there is a holomorphic section $s$ such that, in those frames, $$\\label{compact:end-limits}\n s\\longrightarrow1\\quad(h\\to+\\infty),\\qquad\n s\\longrightarrow0\\quad(h\\to-\\infty),$$ with exponentially decaying errors. Its effective divisor $D=(s=0)$ is contained in $\\{h\\leq H\\}$ for some $H$, is locally finite, and represents the ordinary Chern class $j(e)$, or equivalently its dual locally finite homology class.",
          "source_id": "PM-060.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $E$ be one of the data in Lemma <a href=\"#compact:classes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"compact:classes\">9</a>, and write $e^2=K_Y\\cdot e=-m$ with $m>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-curve-area",
        "exact_quote": "There is a number $A>0$, depending only on a fixed finite coordinate atlas of $X$, its lifted metric, and the covering height, with the following property. Let $E$ be any compact datum whose holomorphic operator tends smoothly to the trivial operator at the negative end, and let $s$ be a nonzero holomorphic section which is bounded in the prescribed smooth trivialization there. If $D=(s=0)$, then $$\\label{curve:area-bound}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Area}}(D\\cap Y_l)\\le C_{E,s}\\exp(Al),\\qquad l\\ge0.$$ The constant $C_{E,s}$ may depend on the datum, operator, and section. The exponent $A$ is independent of their decay rate. The same exponent applies to every reduced component of $D$ and each of its fixed deck translates. Replacing $I_l$ by any fixed-width enlargement also preserves the exponent.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },

The exponent $A$ is independent of their decay rate. The same exponent applies to every reduced component of $D$ and each of its fixed deck translates. Replacing $I_l$ by any fixed-width enlargement also preserves the exponent.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-weighted-currents",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\varepsilon>0$ and $b>\\varepsilon$. Let $F$ be either $\\mathcal O_Y$ or an end-framed compact datum $E$ with an integrable operator satisfying <a href=\"#weighted:end-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"weighted:end-error\">[weighted:end-error]</a>. Consider the current complex $$\\label{weighted:current-complex}\n \\mathcal C^{2,q}_{-\\varepsilon,b,-3}(F)\n =W^{-3-q}_{-\\varepsilon,b}(\\Lambda^{2,q}\\otimes F),\n \\qquad q=0,1,2.$$ Its cohomology is finite-dimensional. For $F=\\mathcal O_Y$ its degree-one cohomology is zero. In particular every closed scalar $(2,1)$-current in this space which is zero on a positive tail is exact.*\n\n*If a degree-zero current $\\beta\\in W^{-3}_{-\\varepsilon,b}(\\Lambda^{2,0}\\otimes F)$ satisfies $\\bar\\partial_F\\beta=J$ for a degree-one current $J$ supported in $\\{h\\le C\\}$, then $\\beta$ vanishes on a positive tail. Finally, an order-zero $(2,1)$-current supported in $\\{h\\le C\\}$ whose masses on negative unit height slabs are at most $C_0e^{\\mu l}$ belongs to this complex whenever $b>\\mu$. The same Sobolev order and pair of rates can be used for any family with that common exponent $\\mu$, regardless of its individual constants and upper support bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },

        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-curve-residue",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $F\\in\\{\\mathcal O,E\\}$ and rates $(-\\varepsilon,b)$ as in Theorem <a href=\"#weighted:currents\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"weighted:currents\">22</a>. For distinct closed irreducible curves $B_i\\subset Y$ bounded above in height, let $\\eta_i$ be nonzero holomorphic differentials on their normalizations with coefficients in $f_i^*F$. Suppose their pushforward currents have negative-end mass exponents strictly smaller than $b$. Then the classes $$[C_{\\eta_i}]\\in H^{2,1}_{-\\varepsilon,b}(Y,F)$$ are linearly independent: every finite subfamily is independent. For $F=\\mathcal O$, even one such differential is impossible.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-curve-residue",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $F\\in\\{\\mathcal O,E\\}$ and rates $(-\\varepsilon,b)$ as in Theorem <a href=\"#weighted:currents\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"weighted:currents\">22</a>.           "source_id": "PM-060.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-curve-genus",
        "exact_quote": "The normalization $R$ of any component of $D$ has no nonzero square-integrable scalar holomorphic differential and has genus zero. If $R$ is compact, it is biholomorphic to $\\mathbb P^1$. If it is noncompact, it is biholomorphic to a domain in $\\mathbb P^1$, without a restriction on the connectivity of that domain.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-curve-genus",
          "exact_quote": "The normalization $R$ of any component of $D$ has no nonzero square-integrable scalar holomorphic differential and has genus zero.           "source_id": "PM-060.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cylinder-small-energy",
        "exact_quote": "There are an integer $P\\geq2$ and positive constants $Q,\\delta,\\varepsilon_0,D_0$, depending only on the lifted geometry, with the following property. Suppose that $\\Omega\\subset\\mathcal C$ is a domain and $f:\\Omega\\to Y$ is a nonconstant proper holomorphic map. A strip $S_j$ is called *flat* if $\\overline{C_j^{2P+2}}\\subset\\Omega$ and the image of this entire thickened cylinder under $f$ is contained in $U_p(r)$ for one of the fixed coordinate charts. Every flat strip satisfies $\\sup_{S_j}u\\leq D_0$.*\n\n\n*For every interval $I\\subset\\mathbb R$, if $S_j$ contains a point $v_j$ with $h(f(v_j))\\in I$ and $S_j$ is not flat, then $$\\label{cylinder:energy-cost}\n \\int_{\\Omega\\cap C_j^Q\\cap(h\\circ f)^{-1}(I+[-\\delta,\\delta])}\n              u^2\\,dA_v\\geq\\varepsilon_0.$$ The same assertion holds on a source half-cylinder whenever $\\overline{C_j^Q}$ lies in its interior.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cylinder-small-energy",
          "exact_quote": "There are an integer $P\\geq2$ and positive constants $Q,\\delta,\\varepsilon_0,D_0$, depending only on the lifted geometry, with the following property. Suppose that $\\Omega\\subset\\mathcal C$ is a domain and $f:\\Omega\\to Y$ is a nonconstant proper holomorphic map. A strip $S_j$ is called *flat* if $\\overline{C_j^{2P+2}}\\subset\\Omega$ and the image of this entire thickened cylinder under $f$ is contained in $U_p(r)$ for one of the fixed coordinate charts. Every flat strip satisfies $\\sup_{S_j}u\\leq D_0$.*\n\n\n*For every interval $I\\subset\\mathbb R$, if $S_j$ contains a point $v_j$ with $h(f(v_j))\\in I$ and $S_j$ is not flat, then $$\\label{cylinder:energy-cost}\n \\int_{\\Omega\\cap C_j^Q\\cap(h\\circ f)^{-1}(I+[-\\delta,\\delta])}\n              u^2\\,dA_v\\geq\\varepsilon_0.$$ The same assertion holds on a source half-cylinder whenever $\\overline{C_j^Q}$ lies in its interior.",
          "source_id": "PM-060.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are an integer $P\\geq2$ and positive constants $Q,\\delta,\\varepsilon_0,D_0$, depending only on the lifted geometry, with the following property. A strip $S_j$ is called *flat* if $\\overline{C_j^{2P+2}}\\subset\\Omega$ and the image of this entire thickened cylinder under $f$ is contained in $U_p(r)$ for one of the fixed coordinate charts. Every flat strip satisfies $\\sup_{S_j}u\\leq D_0$.*\n\n\n*For every interval $I\\subset\\mathbb R$, if $S_j$ contains a point $v_j$ with $h(f(v_j))\\in I$ and $S_j$ is not flat, then $$\\label{cylinder:energy-cost}\n \\int_{\\Omega\\cap C_j^Q\\cap(h\\circ f)^{-1}(I+[-\\delta,\\delta])}\n              u^2\\,dA_v\\geq\\varepsilon_0.$$ The same assertion holds on a source half-cylinder whenever $\\overline{C_j^Q}$ lies in its interior.",
      "explanation": "Properness and analytic-cycle compactness force low-energy thickened source cylinders into coordinate charts; nonflat strips have a uniform positive energy cost.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cylinder-flat-chains",
        "exact_quote": "Let $J\\subset\\mathbb Z$ be a maximal interval of flat indices for a map as in Lemma <a href=\"#cylinder:small-energy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cylinder:small-energy\">25</a>. Put $m_j=\\sup_{S_j}u$. There is a constant $L_0$, depending only on the fixed geometry, such that $$\\label{cylinder:chain-sum}\n \\sum_{j\\in J}m_j\\leq L_0.$$ A doubly infinite chain is impossible for a nonconstant map. If a chain contains a point with height in an interval $I$, every finite endpoint of the chain has a neighboring nonflat strip containing a point with height in $I+[-\\Lambda,\\Lambda]$, where $\\Lambda=\\|dh\\|_\\infty L_0$ is fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

If a chain contains a point with height in an interval $I$, every finite endpoint of the chain has a neighboring nonflat strip containing a point with height in $I+[-\\Lambda,\\Lambda]$, where $\\Lambda=\\|dh\\|_\\infty L_0$ is fixed.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cylinder-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $R_0,C_0>0$ depending only on the lifted geometry with the following property. Let $f:\\Omega\\to Y$ be a nonconstant proper holomorphic map from a domain in $\\mathcal C$. Let $w\\geq0$ be measurable and suppose $$M_w:=\\sup_{j\\in\\mathbb Z}\\|w\\|_{L^2(\\Omega\\cap S_j)}<\\infty.$$ For every interval $I$ of length one, set $$E_I=\\int_{\\Omega\\cap(h\\circ f)^{-1}(I+[-R_0,R_0])}\n                          u^2\\,dA_v.$$ If $E_I<\\infty$, then $$\\label{cylinder:estimate-formula}\n \\int_{\\Omega\\cap(h\\circ f)^{-1}(I)}u\\,w\\,dA_v\n            \\leq C_0M_w(1+E_I).$$ The same estimate holds on a full source half-cylinder cut from a proper normalization map; properness is used only on thickened strips away from the artificial initial boundary, and a fixed finite initial block of strips is included in the constant. The integral on the left is always restricted to the inverse image of the target interval $I$.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cylinder-estimate",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $R_0,C_0>0$ depending only on the lifted geometry with the following property. Let $w\\geq0$ be measurable and suppose $$M_w:=\\sup_{j\\in\\mathbb Z}\\|w\\|_{L^2(\\Omega\\cap S_j)}<\\infty.$$ For every interval $I$ of length one, set $$E_I=\\int_{\\Omega\\cap(h\\circ f)^{-1}(I+[-R_0,R_0])}\n                          u^2\\,dA_v.$$ If $E_I<\\infty$, then $$\\label{cylinder:estimate-formula}\n \\int_{\\Omega\\cap(h\\circ f)^{-1}(I)}u\\,w\\,dA_v\n            \\leq C_0M_w(1+E_I).$$ The same estimate holds on a full source half-cylinder cut from a proper normalization map; properness is used only on thickened strips away from the artificial initial boundary, and a fixed finite initial block of strips is included in the constant. The integral on the left is always restricted to the inverse image of the target interval $I$.",
          "source_id": "PM-060.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cylinder-gauge",
        "exact_quote": "If $a\\in L^1(\\mathcal C)\\cap L^2(\\mathcal C)$, there is a periodic distribution $g$ satisfying $$\\tfrac12(\\partial_t+i\\partial_\\theta)g=a,\n \\qquad\n \\sup_{j\\in\\mathbb Z}\\|g\\|_{W^{1,2}(S_j)}\n       \\leq C\\bigl(\\|a\\|_{L^1}+\\|a\\|_{L^2}\\bigr).$$ For every $p<\\infty$ this solution satisfies $$\\label{cylinder:gauge-exp}\n \\sup_j\\int_{S_j}\\exp\\bigl(p|\\operatorname{Re}g|\\bigr)dA_v<\\infty.$$ If $a$ is smooth in an open set, $g$ is smooth there.",
        "source_id": "PM-060.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-060.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **class VII surface** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A compact complex surface is of *class VII* if its first Betti number is one and its Kodaira dimension is $-\infty$.
- **global spherical shell** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A *global spherical shell* in a complex surface is a holomorphically embedded neighborhood of the unit three-sphere in $\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\}$ whose complement is connected.
- **primary Hopf surface** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A primary Hopf surface is a compact quotient of $\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\}$ by the infinite cyclic group generated by a holomorphic contraction.
- **rational curve** ([source](#sec:surfaces)): A rational curve is a curve whose normalization is $\mathbb P^1$; the curve itself is allowed to be singular.
- **canonical bundle notation** ([source](#sec:surfaces)): We use $K_X$ both for the canonical line bundle and for its first Chern class when a cohomological operation makes the meaning unambiguous.
- **character bundle** ([source](#sec:surfaces)): For $\lambda\in\mathbb C^*$, let $P_\lambda$ be the flat holomorphic line bundle whose sections pull back to holomorphic functions with $f(Ty)=\lambda f(y)$.
- **compact line-bundle datum** ([source](#sec:compact-data)): Here a datum is a smooth complex line bundle $E$ together with a fixed nowhere-zero frame outside a compact set, or, equivalently, fixed frames on both sufficiently distant ends.
- **relative first Chern class** ([source](#sec:compact-data)): It has a relative first Chern class $$e=c_1(E,\text{end frames})\in H_c^2(Y;\mathbb Z).$$
- **admissible pair** ([source](#pm-h-13)): We call a pair admissible when $a\ne0$ and $b\ne0$.
- **source cylinder** ([source](#pm-h-24)): Write $\mathcal C=\mathbb R\times(\mathbb R/2\pi\mathbb Z)$, with coordinate $v=t+i\theta$ and flat area $dA_v=dt\,d\theta$.
- **Laurent intersection pairing** ([source](#conclusion:sesquilinearity)): Define $$\label{conclusion:laurent-pairing} P(a,b)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}\langle a,S_!^k b\rangle t^k \quad(a,b\in H_c^2(Y;\mathbb Q)).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| first-betti | b_1(X) | = | $1$ | Original minimal class VII surface. |
| positive-second-betti | b_2(X) | > | $0$ | Original minimal class VII surface. |
| holomorphic-euler | \chi(\mathcal O_X) | = | $0$ | Original minimal class VII surface. |
| canonical-square | K_X^2 | = | $-b_2(X)$ | Integral intersection products modulo torsion. |
| aspherical-bound | \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S) | ≥ | $\frac95\|\sigma(S)\|$ | Complex orientation and contractible topological universal cover. |
| cyclic-betti | b_2(X_n) | = | $n b_2(X)$ | Finite cyclic quotient. |
| compact-rank | d | ≥ | $n b_2(X)-C_0$ | Independent compact classes with fixed seam. |
| compact-square | e_i^2 | = | $-m_i$ | Sums of distinct canonical basis elements with end frames. |
| index | \sum_{q=0}^2(-1)^q\dim H^{0,q}_{a,b}(Y,E) | = | $1+\frac12\bigl(e^2-K_Y\cdot e\bigr)$ | Compact datum in a cut-open finite cyclic period. |
| area-growth | \mathop{\mathrm{Area}}(D\cap Y_l) | ≤ | $C_{E,s}\exp(Al)$ | Negative unit height slabs; exponent fixed by geometry. |
| correction-rate | \gamma | > | $2A+10$ | Finite prescribed correction exponent after area exponent is fixed. |
| strip-thickening | P | ≥ | $2$ | Integer chosen large enough for coordinate contraction. |
| contraction | q | ≤ | $\frac12$ | Coordinate comparison constant fixed by lifted geometry. |
| chain-sum | \sum_{j\in J}m_j | ≤ | $L_0$ | Maximal interval of flat indices for a proper nonconstant map. |
| slab-mass | \int_{\Omega\cap(h\circ f)^{-1}(I)}u\,w\,dA_v | ≤ | $C_0M_w(1+E_I)$ | Proper map, stripwise bounded multiplier, target interval of length one. |
| rational-lift-orbits | number of compact lift orbits | ≤ | $nr$ | A finite collection of rational curves and finite period translation. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **reduction** [claim](#stmt-surface-reduction) / [proof](#surface:no-square-zero): Separate the effective square-zero case from the negative-divisor case. -> The remaining effective divisors have negative square.
- **compact** [claim](#stmt-compact-classes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compact-classes): Group basis restrictions by torsion order, cut trivialized bundles at the seam, and retain independent sums. -> Independent compact classes with equal negative intersection products.
- **weighted** [claim](#stmt-weighted-fredholm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-weighted-fredholm): Use character acyclicity and Fourier transformation to construct periodic inverses and two-ended parametrices. -> Finite-dimensional weighted cohomology with derivative-gaining homotopies.
- **sections** [claim](#stmt-compact-section) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compact-section): Correct bundle curvature, apply compact index excision, normalize the positive coefficient, and exclude a nonzero negative coefficient. -> Effective locally finite divisors bounded above in height.
- **area** [claim](#stmt-curve-area) / [proof](#proof-stmt-curve-area): Propagate logarithmic bounds through coordinate balls and apply local divisor integration. -> A common geometric area exponent.
- **residues** [claim](#stmt-curve-genus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-curve-genus): Place residue currents in fixed weighted spaces and exclude square-integrable scalar differentials. -> Genus-zero normalizations, planar when open.
- **cylinder** [claim](#stmt-cylinder-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cylinder-estimate): Combine nonflat energy costs with angular-mode contraction on flat chains. -> A target-slab bound for stripwise bounded multipliers.
- **components** [claim](#stmt-cylinder-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cylinder-compactness): Exclude open planar domains using scalar residues, then exclude planes using a periodic gauge and independent translated currents. -> Every divisor component is compact rational.
- **count** [claim](#stmt-conclusion-curves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conclusion-curves): Periodize compact classes and use the nonsingular Laurent pairing against curve-orbit equations. -> The surface has the required lower bound on rational curves.
- **shell** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conclusion-upper-bound-2): Combine the curve lower bound with independence and invoke the rational-curve shell criterion. -> A global spherical shell with connected complement.
- **consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-topology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conclusion-upper-bound-3): Apply shell deformation theory and the topology of point blowups. -> The fundamental group and oriented diffeomorphism type.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-subset-rank**: Sums of $k_\tau$ distinct basis lifts span the full rational coordinate space when $N_\tau>k_\tau$.
- **I-boundary-rank**: When $N_\tau=k_\tau$, the single sum loses $k_\tau-1$ dimensions; when $N_\tau<k_\tau$, no sum loses at most $k_\tau-1$ dimensions.
- **I-shell**: The surface admits a holomorphically embedded spherical neighborhood with connected complement.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-geometric-transfer**: Apply the shell conclusion to software dependency graphs. -> A graph rank computation supplies no complex surface or holomorphic embedding.
- **A-divisor**: Treat the subset-span calculation as construction of an effective divisor. -> A finite cohomology calculation omits the holomorphic section and compactness arguments.
- **A-test-scope**: Treat finite rank tests as verification of the shell theorem. -> Finite coordinate matrices do not decide analytic continuation or compactness of divisor components.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The compact-class argument contains a finite construction of sums of distinct basis elements and a rational span calculation. This implementation covers that algebraic subresult; it does not construct a global spherical shell or decide the weighted analytic assertions.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON integers N and k.
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= N <= 6 and 1 <= k <= 7; standard coordinate basis over Q.
# OUTPUTS: Exact rational rank of all k-subset incidence vectors.
# TIME: Enumerates binomial(N,k) rows; exact elimination on that finite matrix.
# SPACE: O(N * binomial(N,k)) rational entries.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic with python-flint fmpq_mat.
# FAILURE: Invalid types or bounds raise ValueError; no floating approximation.
import json, sys
from itertools import combinations
from flint import fmpq_mat
x = json.load(sys.stdin)
N, k = x['N'], x['k']
if type(N) is not int or type(k) is not int or not (0 <= N <= 6 and 1 <= k <= 7):
    raise ValueError('outside finite contract')
rows = [[int(i in subset) for i in range(N)] for subset in combinations(range(N), k)]
rank = int(fmpq_mat(rows).rank()) if rows else 0
print(rank)
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Shell existence requires a connected compact complex surface satisfying the displayed Betti and Kodaira conditions and minimality. Blocked use: ['Applying shell existence to arbitrary manifolds or discrete dependency graphs']
- **L-square-zero** (edge_case): The effective square-zero divisor case is settled separately; subsequent curve independence and counting use its exclusion. Blocked use: ['Using the curve-class upper bound without excluding effective square-zero divisors']
- **L-rational-singular** (edge_case): Rationality refers to the normalization and permits singular curves. Blocked use: ['Replacing rational curves by smooth embedded spheres without further justification']
- **L-relative-frames** (hypothesis): End frames retain the compact support information. Ordinary Chern classes alone do not recover the specified relative datum. Blocked use: ['Discarding end trivializations when transferring compact classes']
- **L-index-domain** (hypothesis): The index formula assumes a compact datum transferred from a cut-open finite cyclic quotient, fixed collar trivializations, an integrable end-decaying operator, and positive end rates. Blocked use: ['Applying the index formula to arbitrary bundles or arbitrary weights']
- **L-admissibility** (hypothesis): Weighted Fredholm conclusions require admissible rates; zero end rates are excluded. Blocked use: ['Treating the exceptional zero rate as covered by the Fredholm assertion']
- **L-tail-asymmetry** (edge_case): Positive-tail uniqueness does not assert negative-tail vanishing. Blocked use: ['Concluding that a zero negative constant makes a section identically zero']
- **L-decay-choice** (hypothesis): The geometric area exponent is fixed before choosing the connection decay rate, which must satisfy the displayed strict bound. Blocked use: ['Using an arbitrary correction rate in the curve compactness argument']
- **L-local-finiteness** (edge_case): Every divisor component is compact rational, but the divisor as a whole may have infinitely many components. Blocked use: ['Replacing a locally finite divisor by an automatically finite or compact divisor']
- **L-fixed-cohomology** (hypothesis): Residue independence is compared in a single coefficient bundle with fixed weights and Sobolev orders. Blocked use: ['Comparing dimensions across changing bundles or weighted spaces']
- **L-nonuniform** (edge_case): Individual current constants may vary among translates; the argument uses a shared exponent rather than a shared norm bound. Blocked use: ['Claiming a uniform bound on all translated current norms']
- **L-proper-cylinder** (hypothesis): The cylinder estimate requires a nonconstant proper holomorphic map, a stripwise bounded multiplier, and finite enlarged-slab energy. Blocked use: ['Applying the estimate to arbitrary sampled maps or omitting properness']
- **L-compact-test** (formal_scope): Curve counting pairs compact test classes with locally finite divisors; it does not define unrestricted pairings of locally finite cycles. Blocked use: ['Multiplying arbitrary infinite divisor sums in the Laurent pairing']
- **L-aspherical** (hypothesis): The Euler–signature consequence assumes a connected closed smooth complex surface with contractible topological universal cover. Blocked use: ['Using the inequality for general graphs, general manifolds, or unproved asphericity']
- **L-finite-check-scope** (formal_scope): Finite exact rank calculations can check the compact-class algebra but do not certify the analytic existence of a shell. Blocked use: ['Promoting finite matrix tests to verification of the complete geometric theorem']
- **L-engineering-transfer** (unknown): A source-supported transfer theorem to engineering reliability, software verification, or deployment is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Using geometric classification as an engineering acceptance rule']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Global Spherical Shell conjecture: every connected minimal compact complex surface of class VII with positive second Betti number contains a global spherical shell. This is a holomorphically embedded neighborhood of the standard three-sphere in $`\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\}`$ whose complement is connected.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

A compact complex surface is of *class VII* if its first Betti number is one and its Kodaira dimension is $-\infty$. The classification of these non-Kähler surfaces divides according to the second Betti number. In the minimal case with $b_2=0$, work of Bogomolov, Li–Yau–Zheng, and Teleman leads to the classification by Hopf and Inoue surfaces <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Bogomolov1976](#ref-Bogomolov1976), [LiYauZheng1994](#ref-LiYauZheng1994), [Teleman1994](#ref-Teleman1994)]. The positive-$b_2$ case is the setting of the Global Spherical Shell conjecture.

A *global spherical shell* in a complex surface is a holomorphically embedded neighborhood of the unit three-sphere in $\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\}$ whose complement is connected. Kato introduced this structure <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Kato1977](#ref-Kato1977)]. Nakamura’s classification program includes the conjecture that every minimal class VII surface with positive second Betti number contains such a shell <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Nakamura1984](#ref-Nakamura1984), Working Hypothesis 5.5]; see also <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Nakamura1989](#ref-Nakamura1989), [Teleman2010](#ref-Teleman2010)]. We prove this assertion.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Global Spherical Shell conjecture). *Let $X$ be a connected compact complex surface with $$b_1(X)=1,\qquad b_2(X)>0,\qquad \kappa(X)=-\infty.$$ Assume that $X$ is minimal: it contains no smooth rational curve of self-intersection $-1$. Then there are an open neighborhood $U$ of $$S^3=\{z\in\mathbb C^2:|z|=1\}
 \quad\text{in }\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\},$$ an open subset $\Sigma\subset X$, and a biholomorphism $\phi:U\longrightarrow\Sigma$ such that $X\setminus\Sigma$ is connected.*

</div>

Kato’s deformation theorem then identifies the deformation behavior and smooth topology of every surface in this range. A primary Hopf surface is a compact quotient of $\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\}$ by the infinite cyclic group generated by a holomorphic contraction.

<a id="stmt-cor-topology"></a>
<div id="cor:topology" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Deformation and smooth topology). *Let $X$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, and put $b=b_2(X)$. There is a small one-parameter deformation $\mathcal X\to\Delta$ over a disk about $0$, with $\mathcal X_0\simeq X$, such that every nonzero fiber is obtained from a primary Hopf surface by exactly $b$ successive point blowups. The centers may be infinitely near. Moreover, $$\pi_1(X)\cong\mathbb Z,\qquad
 X\cong_{\mathrm{or}}(S^1\times S^3)\#\,b\,\overline{\mathbb{CP}}^{\,2},$$ where the second is an orientation-preserving diffeomorphism for the complex orientation of $X$.*

</div>

The theorem also removes the possible class VII exception in the geography of aspherical complex surfaces studied by Albanese–Di Cerbo–Lombardi <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[AlbaneseDiCerboLombardi2025](#ref-AlbaneseDiCerboLombardi2025)]. Here asphericity means that the topological universal cover is contractible.

<a id="stmt-cor-aspherical-geography"></a>
<div id="cor:aspherical-geography" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (A sharpened Euler–signature inequality). *Let $S$ be a connected closed smooth complex surface whose topological universal cover is contractible. Write $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)$ for its Euler characteristic and $\sigma(S)$ for its signature in the complex orientation. Then $$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)\geq\frac95|\sigma(S)|.$$ If $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)>0$, then $S$ is of general type; otherwise $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)=\sigma(S)=0$.*

</div>

The proofs of these consequences are given in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">7.1</a>. We turn to the geometric reductions and the analytic construction that prove the main theorem.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Curves, shells, and earlier approaches

The shell construction makes the positive-$b_2$ classification problem concrete: one modifies a ball and glues its boundary neighborhoods by a holomorphic embedding. Kato introduced the shell framework <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Kato1977](#ref-Kato1977)]; Dloussky developed its description by sequences of blowups and contracting germs <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Dloussky1984](#ref-Dloussky1984)]. The conjecture asks whether an arbitrary minimal class VII surface with positive second Betti number admits this geometry, even when no curves are initially known to exist.

Two curve criteria reduce the task. Enoki’s theorem handles the presence of a nonzero effective divisor of square zero <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Enoki1981](#ref-Enoki1981)]. In the remaining case, Dloussky, Oeljeklaus, and Toma prove that $b_2(X)$ rational curves imply a global spherical shell <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DOT2003](#ref-DOT2003)]. Throughout this paper a rational curve may be singular: its normalization is $\mathbb P^1$. These criteria shift the burden to producing sufficiently many rational curves on a surface whose intersection form is negative definite. A topological cohomology class alone does not provide an effective divisor, and an effective divisor on a noncompact cover need not have compact components.

Gauge theory has supplied major advances in this curve-production problem. Teleman proved the shell conjecture for $b_2=1$ and proved that a minimal class VII surface with $b_2=2$ contains a cycle of curves <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Teleman2005](#ref-Teleman2005), [Teleman2010](#ref-Teleman2010)]. The cycle conclusion is distinct from the general criterion requiring $b_2$ rational curves. Dloussky’s numerically anticanonical and later twisted-logarithmic-form criteria give further routes from additional geometric structure to a shell <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Dloussky2006](#ref-Dloussky2006), [Dloussky2025](#ref-Dloussky2025)]. Kurnosov and Spicer obtain the shell conclusion for $b_2=3$ when two distinct singular holomorphic foliations are present <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[KurnosovSpicer2026](#ref-KurnosovSpicer2026)].

Infinite divisors on covers also occur in the deformation work of Dloussky and Teleman <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[DlousskyTeleman2012](#ref-DlousskyTeleman2012)]. For families of surfaces with shells, they describe lifted exceptional divisors whose limits can be infinite sums of compact curves. Their extension theorem for more general degenerations still supplies effective divisors, but leaves open whether open Riemann surfaces can occur as components. This distinction is central here. We construct divisors directly on the infinite cyclic cover of the given surface, and prove that every component is compact. The estimates must work for all the compact Chern classes needed in the eventual curve count.

The weighted analysis uses the Fourier–Laplace method for periodic ends developed by Taubes <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Taubes1987](#ref-Taubes1987)]; the treatment of elliptic complexes by Mrowka–Ruberman–Saveliev <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[MRS2014](#ref-MRS2014)] provides a related framework. We prove the Dolbeault statements needed here from acyclicity of compact character bundles, including the continuation through the trivial character. For compactness of curve components, the argument combines residue currents, analytic-cycle compactness <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Bishop1964](#ref-Bishop1964), [HarveyShiffman1974](#ref-HarveyShiffman1974)], and Fourier estimates on source cylinders. A periodic gauge estimate uses exponential integrability <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Trudinger1967](#ref-Trudinger1967)] to control the remaining plane case.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The argument

After Enoki’s reduction we assume that no nonzero effective divisor has square zero. Let $Y\to X$ be the infinite cyclic cover associated with a primitive integral class in $H^1(X;\mathbb Z)$, and let $T$ generate its deck group. We choose a proper height $h$ satisfying $h\circ T=h+1$, with positive constant complex Laplacian for a lifted Gauduchon metric. The positive and negative ends mean $h\to+\infty$ and $h\to-\infty$, respectively. Write $K_Y$ for the canonical bundle and also for its first Chern class in intersection products.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Compact classes and holomorphic sections.

A compact line-bundle datum is a smooth line bundle $E\to Y$ with specified trivializations outside a compact set. Those trivializations define a relative Chern class $e\in H_c^2(Y;\mathbb Z)$. The finite cyclic quotient $X_n=Y/\langle T^n\rangle$ has a negative orthonormal intersection basis of size $n b_2(X)$. Suitable sums of its basis classes become trivial at a fixed cutting hypersurface. Cutting there gives $d\ge n b_2(X)-C_0$ independent compact classes $e_i$, with $C_0$ independent of $n$, and $$e_i^2=K_Y\cdot e_i<0.$$ Section <a href="#sec:compact-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:compact-data">3</a> constructs these data and retains their prescribed end trivializations.

The absence of effective square-zero divisors makes the nontrivial character-twisted Dolbeault complexes on $X$ acyclic. In Section <a href="#sec:weighted" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:weighted">4</a>, Fourier transformation in the deck variable then gives Fredholm complexes on $Y$ with exponential conditions at both ends. Each compact datum admits a holomorphic structure approaching the trivial operator at any prescribed finite exponential rate. For the classes above, compact excision gives weighted index one. The remaining ingredients are degree-two vanishing and uniqueness from the positive-end constant coefficient: together they force a one-dimensional section space. Normalize its section $s$ to approach one at the positive end. Its negative-end constant must be zero; otherwise its divisor would be compact, have relative class $e$, and project to an effective divisor of negative canonical degree on $X$. Thus the zero divisor $D=(s=0)$ is bounded above in height.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Differentials obstruct open components.

The components of $D$ are not yet known to be compact. First, Section <a href="#sec:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:curves">5</a> bounds the area of $D$ on the height slabs $[-\ell-1,-\ell]$, $\ell\ge0$, by $C_D e^{A\ell}$, with $A$ depending only on the fixed geometry. The holomorphic correction rate can therefore be chosen sufficiently large after $A$ is fixed. If $f:R\to B\subset Y$ is the proper normalization of a component, a holomorphic differential on $R$ defines a $(2,1)$-current on $Y$ by integration against pulled-back test forms. Exponential mass bounds place these currents in one fixed weighted cohomology space.

There are two different cohomological obstructions. For scalar currents the degree-one cohomology group is zero. An exact residue current has a primitive vanishing on the positive end; analytic continuation forces that primitive to be supported on the curve, where a local distribution calculation rules it out. In particular there are no nonzero $L^2$ holomorphic differentials on $R$, so $R$ has genus zero. A compact normalization is therefore $\mathbb P^1$, and an open one is a domain in $\mathbb P^1$.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Two uses of the cylinder estimate.

Section <a href="#sec:cylinder" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cylinder">6</a> proves a bound for a differential’s mass over a target height slab in terms of the curve’s area over a fixed enlarged slab. On the logarithmic source cylinder, with coordinate $v=t+i\theta$ and $\theta$ taken modulo $2\pi$, it allows a multiplier with uniformly bounded $L^2$ norm on each unit $t$-strip. Its first application uses multiplier one: if the domain $R\subset\mathbb P^1$ omits two points, put them at $0$ and $\infty$ and use $dz/z$ on the resulting domain in $\mathbb C^*$. The estimate gives an exponentially bounded scalar residue current, contradicting the preceding obstruction. An open normalization must consequently be the plane.

For a plane component $B$, consider translates $B_k=T^kB$ that are not contained in $D$, with normalization maps $f_k:\mathbb C\to B_k$. The bundle-valued differentials $(f_k^*s)\,dz$ are nonzero. Properness and the upper height bound send $|z|\to\infty$ to the negative end, where $s\to0$. A periodic gauge converts this limit into a holomorphic function vanishing to order at least one at infinity, canceling the growth of $dz$ in the coordinate $v=\log z$. The same cylinder estimate, now with an $L^2$-bounded multiplier supplied by the gauge, bounds all these currents with the same exponent. Their coefficients lie in one fixed bundle $E$. Its degree-one current cohomology is finite dimensional, and the residue argument makes currents on distinct curves linearly independent. Infinitely many translates give the contradiction.

The uniform cylinder bound is what permits both applications. Its proof separates source strips having a fixed energy cost from long chains whose images lie in small coordinate charts. Along each such chain, the derivative has zero constant angular Fourier mode. The remaining modes give a summable derivative bound independent of the chain length, so the change of target height along the chain is uniformly bounded. No bound on the moduli of thin necks is required.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Counting curves downstairs.

Every component of every $D_i$ is now compact rational, although a whole divisor can still have infinitely many components. In Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">7</a>, their intersections with compact test classes are organized over $\mathbb Q[t,t^{-1}]$, where $t$ records translation by $T^n$. The pairing of the classes $e_i$ becomes nonsingular over $\mathbb Q(t)$. If there were $r<b_2(X)$ rational curves downstairs, their lifts would give at most $nr$ translation orbits, too few to support $d\ge n b_2(X)-C_0$ independent classes for large $n$. The resulting lower bound, together with the curve-class upper bound when no effective divisor has square zero, gives exactly $b_2(X)$ rational curves. Dloussky–Oeljeklaus–Toma then supplies the shell.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Organization and conventions

Section <a href="#sec:surfaces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:surfaces">2</a> establishes the compact-surface facts, the height, and the finite cyclic quotients. Section <a href="#sec:compact-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:compact-data">3</a> constructs the compact Chern classes, and Section <a href="#sec:weighted" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:weighted">4</a> produces their holomorphic sections. Section <a href="#sec:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:curves">5</a> develops the growth and residue obstructions. Section <a href="#sec:cylinder" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cylinder">6</a> proves the cylinder estimate and compactness of every divisor component. Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">7</a> counts the curves, proves the main theorem, and derives the two introductory consequences.

All intersection products are taken with the complex orientation. Integral cohomology classes used in diagonal bases are understood modulo torsion; prescribed end trivializations retain the compact support information on the cover. The complex Laplacian has the subharmonic sign. Unless stated otherwise, geometric norms on the cover come from a fixed Hermitian metric on the compact base.

<a id="sec:surfaces"></a>
### Surface invariants and the cyclic cover

Throughout, a curve means a reduced irreducible compact analytic curve unless another meaning is specified. A rational curve is a curve whose normalization is $\mathbb P^1$; the curve itself is allowed to be singular. We orient every complex surface by its complex structure, and write a dot for the intersection pairing. We use $K_X$ both for the canonical line bundle and for its first Chern class when a cohomological operation makes the meaning unambiguous.

<a id="surface:invariants"></a>
The compact-surface identities of Kodaira give, under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, $$\label{surface:invariants}
 h^{0,1}(X)=1,\qquad h^{1,0}(X)=h^{2,0}(X)=0,\qquad
 b_2^+(X)=0,\qquad \chi(\mathcal O_X)=0,
 \qquad K_X^2=-b_2(X).$$ These are the numerical identities for class VII surfaces <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Kodaira1964](#ref-Kodaira1964), Theorem 3]; see also <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[BHPV2004](#ref-BHPV2004)]. In particular the intersection form on $H^2(X;\mathbb R)$ is negative definite. Our two surface-theoretic reductions are the following established results.

<a id="stmt-surface-reduction"></a>
<div id="surface:reduction" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Surface reductions). *Let $V$ be a minimal compact complex surface with $b_1(V)=1$, $\kappa(V)=-\infty$, and $b_2(V)>0$.*

1.  *If $V$ has a nonzero effective divisor $C$ with $C^2=0$, then $V$ has a global spherical shell.*

2.  *If $V$ has exactly $b_2(V)$ rational curves, then $V$ has a global spherical shell.*

*Here the conclusion supplies an embedded open neighborhood of $S^3\subset\mathbb C^2\setminus\{0\}$ with connected complement in $V$.*

</div>

<a id="surface:no-square-zero"></a>
The first assertion is the square-zero-divisor case of Enoki’s theorem <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Enoki1981](#ref-Enoki1981)], stated in this form in <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Teleman2010](#ref-Teleman2010), p. 1758]. The second is the Main Theorem of Dloussky–Oeljeklaus–Toma <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[DOT2003](#ref-DOT2003), p. 283]; their definition on p. 284 includes the connected-complement condition. These statements impose no condition on the fundamental group beyond the displayed surface hypotheses. The original surface $X$ satisfies all of them. We may therefore assume henceforth that $$\label{surface:no-square-zero}
 C^2<0\quad\text{for every nonzero effective divisor }C\text{ on }X.$$ Indeed negative definiteness leaves only the already settled square-zero case as an alternative.

<a id="stmt-surface-degree"></a>
<div id="surface:degree" class="lemma">

<a id="surface:canonical-basis"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Canonical basis and curve degrees). *Put $b=b_2(X)$. There is a basis $u_1,\ldots,u_b$ of $H^2(X;\mathbb Z)/\operatorname{Tors}$ with $$\label{surface:canonical-basis}
 u_i\cdot u_j=-\delta_{ij},\qquad
 K_X=u_1+\cdots+u_b\pmod{\operatorname{Tors}}.$$ For every curve $B\subset X$, <a id="surface:curve-degrees"></a>$$\label{surface:curve-degrees}
 B^2<0,\qquad (K_X+B)\cdot B\leq0,\qquad K_X\cdot B\geq0.$$ Consequently every effective divisor has nonnegative canonical degree.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-surface-degree"><summary>Proof of stmt-surface-degree</summary>

*Proof.* Donaldson’s diagonalization theorem in its arbitrary-fundamental-group version applies to the smooth, closed, oriented four-manifold $X$, whose intersection form is negative definite by <a href="#surface:invariants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="surface:invariants">[surface:invariants]</a>. Precisely, we use Theorem 1 of <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Donaldson1987](#ref-Donaldson1987)], on integral cohomology modulo torsion. Its surface application is also recorded in <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Teleman2010](#ref-Teleman2010), 1.1, p. 1756]. Choose a negative orthonormal integral basis and write $K_X=\sum_i k_i u_i$ modulo torsion. The canonical class is characteristic: its mod-two reduction is the second Stiefel–Whitney class. Thus every $k_i$ is odd. Since $K_X^2=-b$, we have $\sum_i k_i^2=b$, so $k_i=\pm1$ for every $i$. Replacing each basis vector by its negative when necessary gives <a href="#surface:canonical-basis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="surface:canonical-basis">[surface:canonical-basis]</a>.

If $[B]=\sum_i a_i u_i$ modulo torsion, the integers $a_i$ give $$(K_X+B)\cdot B=-\sum_i a_i(a_i+1)\leq0.$$ The strict inequality $B^2<0$ follows from <a href="#surface:no-square-zero" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="surface:no-square-zero">[surface:no-square-zero]</a>. The adjunction formula for a possibly singular integral curve is $$K_X\cdot B=2p_a(B)-2-B^2.$$ If $p_a(B)\geq1$, this is positive. If $p_a(B)=0$, the normalization-genus formula $p_a(B)=g(\widetilde B)+\sum_x\delta_x$ shows that $B$ is a smooth rational curve.
